## **SECTION 0 — PREREQUISITES & HOW TO USE**

### **Prerequisites:**

**→ REQUIREMENT: Block I complete** (Docs 1-4)

**Especially critical:**

✅ **Doc 4** (Offer engineered) — the blueprints here PRESENT an offer (they don't create it — the offer already exists from Doc 4)

✅ **Doc 3** (Big Idea, Voice) — the copy EXPRESSES positioning (the Big Idea permeates the structure, the Voice = tone)

✅ **Doc 2** (State/Stage) — DETERMINES which blueprint to choose (the Decision Tree in Section 1.7 uses State/Stage as input)

**Optional but useful:**

Doc 1 (Laws) — understand WHY the structures work (Axiom 1: copy = salesmanship, sales structure = copy structure).

### **Time:**

**Complete read-through:** 4-5 hours (Part I principles + Part II 8 blueprints).

**Practical use:** 30min-2h (choose format with Decision Tree → go directly to the specific blueprint → follow the structure → write the copy).

### **How to Use This Document:**

**FIRST TIME (Copywriter Learning):**

1.  Part I completely (universal principles — the foundation for all formats)
2.  Part II: Read ALL 8 blueprints (understand the options, when to use each)
3.  Decision Tree (learn how to choose)
4.  Practice: Choose 1 blueprint, write copy following it

**PRACTICAL USE (Copywriter Executing a Project):**

1.  **Decision Tree** (Section 1.7 — 5 min): Inputs (State/Stage from Doc 2, Price, Goal) → Output (recommended format)
2.  **Specific Blueprint** (Section 2.X.Y — 15-30 min read): Structure, components, sequence
3.  **Checklist** (Part III — 10 min): Validate during/after writing
4.  **Write** (following the blueprint — 2-8h depending on format)

**Total:** 30 min prep + 2-8h execution = complete copy in the appropriate format.

### **Output:**

**Complete, ready-to-use copy:**

-   Sales Letter (6-10k words)
-   VSL Script (30-45min, 5-8k words)
-   Webinar (60-90min outline + slides)
-   Email (150-400 words)
-   Ad (15-90 second script)
-   Landing page (300-800 words)
-   Etc.

### **Connection to Other Docs:**

**Doc 5 (this one)** = STRUCTURES (blueprints, scaffolding, architecture).

**Docs 6-7** = COMPONENTS (headlines, bullets — fill the blueprint sections).

**Doc 8** = PROOF (testimonials, data — the proof section of blueprints).

**Doc 9** = LANGUAGE (words, tone — polish the copy).

**Docs 10-13** = SYSTEMS (funnels, sequences, optimization, swipes).

**Workflow:**

Doc 5 (choose structure) → Doc 6 (write headline) → Doc 7 (bullets) → Doc 8 (proof) → Doc 9 (polish language) → Launch.

**Structure first** (foundation), components next (fill), polish last.

## **PART I — UNIVERSAL STRUCTURAL PRINCIPLES**

## **SECTION 1.1 — THE 5 FUNDAMENTALS (SCHWAB)**

**Victor Schwab** (How to Write a Good Advertisement, 1962 — a classic, tested for 60+ years, still valid in 2024):

> _"Every successful advertisement, regardless of format or medium, does 5 things._
> 
> _Miss one = the copy is incomplete (a gap)._
> 
> _Do all 5 = the copy is functional (the foundation is solid)."_

**THE 5 FUNDAMENTALS (Apply to ANY Format):**

### **FUNDAMENTAL #1 — GET ATTENTION (Capture in 5 Seconds):**

**Component:** Headline (ads, letters, pages) OR Hook (VSL, webinar) OR Subject line (email).

**Objective:** STOP the scroll/skim (capture the attention of someone passing by quickly).

**Timeframe:** 5-second decision (Schwab tested — the audience decides to continue/skip in 5 sec on average).

**How to capture attention:**

**A. The 4 U's** (Bly — developed in Doc 6):

-   **Useful:** A clear benefit (solves a specific problem)
-   **Urgent:** Action now (not later)
-   **Unique:** Differentiated (not generic, seen 100x before)
-   **Ultra-specific:** Numbers, details (not vague)

**Score:** 30+/40 points = strong headline (Doc 6 checklist).

**B. Curiosity gap** (Heath, Doc 1):

-   Incompleteness (a question without an answer, a revelation without the reveal)
-   The brain MUST resolve it (clicks/reads to close the gap)

**C. Big Idea** (Doc 3):

-   A fresh angle (counter-intuitive, a discovery, a conspiracy)
-   Cuts through saturation (especially in Stage 4-5)

**Validation test:**

Show the headline/hook to 5 people from your target audience.

**Ask:** "Does this make you want to read more/know more?"

✅ 4-5/5 "YES": Attention captured (strong).

⚠️ 2-3/5: Moderate (refine).

❌ 0-1/5: Weak (rewrite completely).

**Ogilvy (historical validation):**

> _"On average, 5x as many people read the headline as read the body copy._
> 
> _80 cents of your advertising dollar is spent on the headline._
> 
> _If the headline fails, the body copy is never read (wasted)."_

**Implication:** Invest 40-60% of your effort/time in the headline (not 10% — proportion matters).

### **FUNDAMENTAL #2 — SHOW THE ADVANTAGE (Clear Benefits):**

**Component:** The benefits section (bullets, subheads, body copy explaining what they gain).

**Objective:** Answer "What's in it for ME?" (WIIFM — the radio station everyone listens to).

**Features vs. Benefits (Translation is Critical):**

**Feature:** What the product HAS/DOES (technical, objective).

Example: "The course has 12 modules, 40 hours of video"

**Benefit:** What the CUSTOMER GAINS by using it (outcome, transformation).

Example: "You will master copywriting → write copy that converts at 5%+ → earn R$10-30k/month as a freelancer"

**Both are necessary:**

**Features:** Explain HOW it works (credibility, logic, mechanism — Doc 3).

**Benefits:** Explain WHY they should care (motivation, desire, transformation — Doc 1 LF8).

**Features alone:** Boring (tech specs, not emotional).

**Benefits alone:** Not credible (a promise without a mechanism).

**Both:** Credible (features) + Desirable (benefits) = persuasive.

**Translation chain** (Doc 3 Section 3.4 — The Drill Analogy):

```
Level 1 — Feature: "12 video modules"
↓
Level 2 — Functional Benefit: "Learn complete copywriting"
↓
Level 3 — Emotional Benefit: "Sell without calls (avoid rejection)"
↓
Level 4 — Aspirational: "R$20k/month, 6h/day, work from the beach"
↓
Level 5 — Identity: "An admired expert (proud family)"
```

**Copy:** Mention features (Level 1-2 for credibility) BUT EMPHASIZE benefits (Levels 3-5 for desire/emotion).

### **FUNDAMENTAL #3 — PROVE IT (Skepticism is the Default, Proof Overcomes It):**

**Component:** The proof section (testimonials, data, case studies, demonstrations, authority, guarantee).

**Objective:** Break skepticism (Doc 1 — they've been burned before, Stage 4-5 cynicism, "prove it or I don't believe you").

**5 Tiers of Proof** (fully developed in Doc 8):

**Tier 1:** Testimonials (social proof — others achieved it, 3-5 specific ones)

**Tier 2:** Data/Track Record (X clients, Y% success, Z timeframe — aggregated results)

**Tier 3:** Demonstrations (before/after, detailed case studies, video proof)

**Tier 4:** Credentials (your authority — education, years, clients, media, expertise)

**Tier 5:** Guarantee (results-based is ideal — Doc 4 Level 4-5, the ultimate proof of confidence)

**Stack ALL 5** (not just 1-2).

Masterson tested: 1 tier (100% baseline) → 5 tiers (+140% conversion) — a cumulative effect.

**Claim size ∝ Proof size** (Bejakovic formula — Doc 8):

Modest claim ("helps you sleep better"): 1-2 testimonials are enough.

Bold claim ("eliminates insomnia in 7 days"): 5+ testimonials + a study + a guarantee + a demonstration (massive proof required).

**A bigger claim requires more proof** (exponentially, not linearly).

### **FUNDAMENTAL #4 — PERSUADE THEM TO GRASP IT (Urgency — Why NOW):**

**Component:** The urgency section (deadline, scarcity, consequences of delay).

**Objective:** Convert "I'll buy it someday" → "I need to buy it NOW" (immediate action).

**Without urgency:**

"Available anytime. Buy whenever you want."

**Result:** Infinite procrastination (Ariely — present bias, "later" = never).

CVR: Baseline (assume 100%).

**With urgency** (Doc 4 Part VI — 7 types):

Deadline (Friday 11:59 PM), scarcity (50 spots), price increase (+R$1k after), opportunity cost (R$2k/week lost by delaying).

**Result:** A forced decision (act now to avoid multiple losses).

CVR: +60-100% (real urgency has been validated for decades).

**Stack 3-4 types** (not a single one — Doc 4 Section 6.3).

Multi-layer urgency = resistance is overwhelmed (so many reasons to act, zero reasons to wait).

**Schwab:**

> _"Urgency converts desire into action._
> 
> _Desire alone = thinking (not buying)._
> 
> _Urgency (a real deadline, genuine scarcity) = immediate action."_

### **FUNDAMENTAL #5 — ASK FOR THE ACTION (A Specific CTA, Not Vague):**

**Component:** The Call-to-Action (an explicit command of what to do, how to do it, and why to do it now).

**Objective:** Direct the action (don't assume the prospect will act on their own — most won't unless EXPLICITLY ASKED).

**Weak CTA** (vague, passive):

❌ "Learn more" (unclear action — more about what? How?)

❌ "Click here" (no benefit, no urgency, generic)

❌ "Contact us" (high friction — what should I say? Email? Call?)

**Strong CTA** (specific, active, benefit + urgency):

✅ "Click the green button below NOW to secure your spot (only 23 left, first-come, first-served)"

Breakdown: Command (click the green button), timing (now), benefit (secure your spot), urgency (23 left), ease (below — location), FOMO (first-come).

✅ "Fill out the form in 60 seconds, schedule a free 30-min call, and find out if you qualify for the program (only 12 spots open this quarter)"

Breakdown: Action (fill out), ease (60 sec), benefit (free call, qualify), exclusivity (12 spots), clear multi-step process (form → call → qualify).

**Elements of a strong CTA:**

1.  **Command** (imperative verb — click, fill out, call, download, watch)
2.  **Specific** (what exactly — the green button, the form, the number)
3.  **Ease** (how easy — 60 sec, 1 click, 30 seconds)
4.  **Benefit** (what they get — secure your spot, free call, get access)
5.  **Urgency** (why now — X left, expires on Y, bonus Z)

**All 5 present:** CTA is maximized (highest conversion).

**CTA Repetition:**

Long-form (8k-word sales letter): CTA 3-5x (after benefits, after proof, after FAQ, before P.S., final P.S.).

**Not annoying** (context is different each time — a logical progression).

Short-form (200w email): CTA 1-2x (mid-email, P.S.).

**Schwab's rule:**

> _"NEVER assume the prospect knows what to do next._
> 
> _TELL them explicitly (command, don't suggest)._
> 
> _Make the action EASY (remove friction, 1-click is ideal)._
> 
> _Give them a REASON to act now (urgency, immediate benefit)."_

```

║ SUMMARY — SCHWAB'S 5 FUNDAMENTALS                 ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
```

## **SECTION 1.2 — THE 4 PILLARS (MASTERSON — Perfect Promo Foundation)**

**Michael Masterson/Mark Ford** (Agora Financial, 20+ years, billions in newsletter revenue):

> _"Every successful promotion (sales letter, VSL, webinar, email campaign) rests on 4 pillars._
> 
> _Remove 1 pillar = the structure collapses (the copy fails)._
> 
> _All 4 strong = the structure stands (the copy succeeds)."_

**THE 4 PILLARS:**

### **PILLAR #1 — PREMISE (Big Idea + Mechanism):**

**What it is:** The central concept (angle, mechanism, WHY the product works).

**Comes from:** Doc 3 (Big Idea, Unique Mechanism — already created).

**In the copy:** Permeates everything (the headline expresses it, the body develops it, the bullets highlight it, the proof validates it).

**Example:**

**Premise:** "Fat burns fat via ketosis (the keto paradox)" — Big Idea + Mechanism integrated.

**Expressed in the copy:**

-   Headline: "Lose Weight by Eating Bacon — The Keto Paradox Tested by 12,000 People"
-   Body: Develops ketosis (the mechanism of HOW fat-burning works)
-   Bullets: "Why fat is your friend (not your enemy) for burning fat"
-   Proof: Keto studies, transformation testimonials

**The Premise is the DNA of the copy** (everything connects back to the central premise).

**Weak Pillar #1:**

A vague premise ("improve your health" — generic), without a mechanism (doesn't explain HOW).

**Result:** Generic copy (not memorable, not differentiated, blends into the saturation).

**Strong Pillar #1:**

A specific premise ("fat burns fat via ketosis"), with a clear mechanism (the science of ketosis explained).

**Result:** Memorable copy (the paradox sticks), differentiated (unique angle), credible (mechanism provides logic).

### **PILLAR #2 — PROMISE (Result + Timeframe + Ease):**

**What it is:** The specific promise (what the client achieves, when, and how easily).

**Comes from:** Doc 4 Part I (Dream Outcome — Value Equation component #1).

**In the copy:** Stated early (headline or lead), reinforced throughout (benefits, proof, guarantee).

**Components of a strong promise:**

1.  **Specific result:** Quantified (20 lbs, R$10k/month, conversationally fluent) — not vague ("improve," "learn")
    
2.  **Timeframe:** When (90 days, 6 months, first results in 60 days) — not indefinite ("eventually")
    
3.  **Ease factor:** How easy (20 min/day, 3 steps, anyone can do it) — not vague ("requires hard work")
    

**Example of a complete promise:**

"You will lose 18-24 lbs in 90 days (3-5 lbs of water weight in the first week, then a steady 1.5-2 lbs/week of fat) by doing keto with 20 min/day of meal prep (simple recipes and templates provided) + a 20-min walk (enjoyable, not HIIT torture) — anyone can do it (if you can cook an egg, you can do this)."

**Result:** 18-24 lbs (specific, honest range).

**Timeframe:** 90 days (clear), with progression (week 1, then weekly).

**Ease:** 20 min prep + 20 min walk = 40 min/day (manageable), simple (cooking an egg = very low barrier).

**Weak Pillar #2:**

A vague promise ("results in some time by doing the work") — everything is indefinite.

**Result:** Not compelling (can't visualize, can't commit).

**Strong Pillar #2:**

A specific promise (numbers, timeline, ease detailed).

**Result:** Compelling (they can visualize the outcome, the timeframe is realistic, the ease makes it feel achievable).

### **PILLAR #3 — PROOF (5-Tier Validation Stack):**

**What it is:** Evidence that the product works (not just a claim — validation).

**Comes from:** Doc 8 (Proof Hierarchy — 5 tiers developed).

**In the copy:** A dedicated proof section (15-25% of long-form copy), interspersed (testimonials throughout), and the guarantee (final proof).

**5 Tiers** (Doc 8 — preview here):

**Tier 1 — Testimonials:** Social proof (others achieved it — 3-5 transformation stories, similar to the avatar).

**Tier 2 — Data:** Track record (X people, Y% success, Z avg result — aggregated validation).

**Tier 3 — Demonstrations:** Case studies (detailed, verifiable), before/after (visual), video (authenticity).

**Tier 4 — Credentials:** Your authority (years, education, clients, media, expertise).

**Tier 5 — Guarantee:** Results-based (Doc 4 Level 4-5 — if you guarantee the result, you must be confident it works).

**Stack:** Use 4-5 tiers (not just testimonials alone — comprehensive proof).

Masterson: 5 stacked tiers = +140% conversion vs. 1 tier.

**Weak Pillar #3:**

Minimal proof (1-2 generic testimonials "it worked, I recommend it — John") — insufficient for high skepticism.

**Result:** Not credible (claim > proof, gap = doubt).

**Strong Pillar #3:**

Abundant proof (5 tiers, specific, diverse, verifiable).

**Result:** Credible (claim is overwhelmingly supported, doubt is dissolved).

### **PILLAR #4 — PRICE (Value Equation Optimized):**

**What it is:** The pricing + offer structure (what you pay, what you get, clear ROI).

**Comes from:** The entirety of Doc 4 (Pricing Strategy Part IV, Offer Stack Part III).

**In the copy:** The offer section (Brunson's stack presentation — Doc 4 Part VII, value before price, contrast).

**Components:**

**A. Value stack:** Core + itemized bonuses (each with an assigned value).

**B. Total value:** The sum (a high anchor — R$5,100).

**C. Price:** Revealed AFTER the value (contrast — R$997).

**D. ROI:** The savings (R$5,100 - R$997 = R$4,103 saved, a 5:1 ratio).

**E. Payment options:** Pay-in-full (save money) + Installments (makes it easier).

**F. Framing:** Daily (R$8/day), monthly (R$250/month), annual (R$2,997) — the smallest unit.

**Weak Pillar #4:**

Price alone ("R$997") without context, without a value stack, without comparison — can seem expensive/arbitrary.

**Result:** Resistance ("it's expensive, not sure if it's worth it").

**Strong Pillar #4:**

Price is contextualized (R$5,100 value BEFORE the R$997 price), ROI is clear (5:1), payment options (make it easier), framing (R$8/day is trivial).

**Result:** Perceived as a bargain (value is overwhelming compared to the price, logic: "this is an obvious steal").
